I can imagine some of it might have been okay, but I haven’t really felt clear enough to tell whether I’d actually enjoy it or just be going through the motions.\n\n[Last Question]\nLooking back, do you think you would still enjoy the things you used to, like hobbies or time with friends, or has that enjoyment gone away completely?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 34 / civil_engineer / bachelors\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Plain patient.\nA plain patient describes their symptoms and experiences in a direct and straightforward manner, providing clear and relevant answers to the doctor's questions.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3, F32.9, F33.0, F33.1, F33.2, F33.3, F33.4, F33.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome. Please have a seat. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I just can’t shake this heavy, hopeless feeling. It feels like everything I’d built back up after my divorce has collapsed, and I’m just… sitting under all of it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: About a month now. It started around when the financial mess from the divorce really hit, and since then it’s just been hanging over me.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or felt no pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy, like hobbies, work, or spending time with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I haven’t paid close attention to that specifically. I know I’ve been feeling really low, but I can’t say clearly whether it’s that I don’t enjoy things or that I just haven’t been doing much.\n\nDoctor: Looking back at the last month, have you found that you don’t take pleasure or feel interested in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies, work, or time with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I’m still not totally sure how to separate that out. I haven’t really been doing much of anything lately, so it’s hard to tell if I don’t enjoy things or if I just haven’t had the energy or focus to try.\n\nDoctor: Even if you haven’t been doing much lately, can you recall if you still would have enjoyed those things if you had done them?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I can imagine some of it might have been okay, but I haven’t really felt clear enough to tell whether I’d actually enjoy it or just be going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, do you think you would still enjoy the things you used to, like hobbies or time with friends, or has that enjoyment gone away completely?\n\nPatient: I really can’t say for sure. I haven’t noticed a clear change exactly, because I haven’t been doing those things much. I haven’t really been looking forward to much, but I also haven’t been trying to plan or do those things, so it’s hard to tell if I care less or if I’m just kind of not in that mindset right now.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, have you felt less of a desire or longing to do the things you used to enjoy, even if you haven’t actually been doing them lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can separate that out. I haven’t really felt a strong pull to do those things, but I don’t know if that means the desire is actually less, or if I’ve just been kind of not thinking about them much.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, have you felt less interested in the things you used to enjoy, even if you haven’t actually been doing them lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed a clear drop in interest, but I also haven’t been doing much of those things, so it’s hard for me to say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, have you felt less interested in the things you used to enjoy, even if you haven’t actually been doing them lately?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I haven’t been thinking about those things much, so I can’t really tell if I’m less interested or if I’m just not engaging with them right now.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, have you felt less interested in the things you used to enjoy, even if you haven’t actually been doing them lately?\n\nPatient: I’m still not really sure. I haven’t felt a clear “yes, I don’t care about that anymore,” but I also haven’t been drawn to those things much lately, so it’s hard for me to tell.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It started around when the financial mess from the divorce really hit, and since then it’s just been hanging over me.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or felt no pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy, like hobbies, work, or spending time with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I haven’t paid close attention to that specifically. I know I’ve been feeling really low, but I can’t say clearly whether it’s that I don’t enjoy things or that I just haven’t been doing much.\n\nDoctor: Looking back at the last month, have you found that you don’t take pleasure or feel interested in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies, work, or time with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I’m still not totally sure how to separate that out. I haven’t really been doing much of anything lately, so it’s hard to tell if I don’t enjoy things or if I just haven’t had the energy or focus to try.\n\nDoctor: Even if you haven’t been doing much lately, can you recall if you still would have enjoyed those things if you had done them?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I can imagine some of it might have been okay, but I haven’t really felt clear enough to tell whether I’d actually enjoy it or just be going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, do you think you would still enjoy the things you used to, like hobbies or time with friends, or has that enjoyment gone away completely?\n\nPatient: I really can’t say for sure. It doesn’t feel like I can clearly tell whether the enjoyment is gone, or whether I just haven’t been in a place to try those things and notice. I know I haven’t been doing much, but I don’t want to say it’s completely gone if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how you feel about the things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends, even if you haven’t been doing them much lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed a clear change exactly, because I haven’t been doing those things much. I haven’t really been looking forward to much, but I also haven’t been trying to plan or do those things, so it’s hard to tell if I care less or if I’m just kind of not in that mindset right now.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, have you felt less of a desire or longing to do the things you used to enjoy, even if you haven’t actually been doing them lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can separate that out. I haven’t really felt a strong pull to do those things, but I don’t know if that means the desire is actually less, or if I’ve just been kind of not thinking about them much.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, have you felt less interested in the things you used to enjoy, even if you haven’t actually been doing them lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed a clear drop in interest, but I also haven’t been doing much of those things, so it’s hard for me to say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, have you felt less interested in the things you used to enjoy, even if you haven’t actually been doing them lately?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I haven’t been thinking about those things much, so I can’t really tell if I’m less interested or if I’m just not engaging with them right now.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, have you felt less interested in the things you used to enjoy, even if you haven’t actually been doing them lately?\n\nPatient: I’m still not really sure.
